fx <- function(...) {
  arg_exprs <- substitute(list(...))[-1]
  i <- 1

  while (i <= length(arg_exprs)) {
    expr <- arg_exprs[[i]]
    is_comment <- FALSE

    # Skip if it's a comment without a preceding dataframe
    if (is.character(expr)) {
      i <- i + 1
      next
    }

    df_name <- deparse(expr)
    comment <- ""

    # Check if next argument is a comment string
    if (i < length(arg_exprs) && is.character(arg_exprs[[i + 1]])) {
      comment <- eval(arg_exprs[[i + 1]], envir = .GlobalEnv)
      i <- i + 1
    }

    # Try to evaluate the dataframe
    df_exists <- exists(df_name, envir = .GlobalEnv)
    if (df_exists) {
      df <- get(df_name, envir = .GlobalEnv)
      if (!inherits(df, "data.frame")) {
        message(df_name, " exists but is not a dataframe. Skipping.")
        i <- i + 1
        next
      }
      col_list <- paste(names(df), collapse = ", ")
    } else {
      col_list <- ""
      comment <- paste0("object '", df_name, "' not found\n", comment)
      message(df_name, " is not present in this current environment but it was added to all_names.")
    }

    # Add or update entry
    idx <- which(all_names$dataframe == df_name)
    if (length(idx) == 1) {
      if (comment != "") {
        all_names$comment[idx] <<- paste(all_names$comment[idx], comment, sep = "\n")
      }
      if (col_list != "") {
        all_names$columns[idx] <<- col_list
      }
    } else {
      all_names <<- rbind(
        all_names,
        data.frame(dataframe = df_name, columns = col_list, comment = comment, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
      )
    }

    i <- i + 1
  }

  # Reorder: move rows with empty columns to bottom
  empty_rows <- which(all_names$columns == "")
  filled_rows <- which(all_names$columns != "")
  all_names <<- all_names[c(filled_rows, empty_rows), ]
}

all_names <- data.frame(
  dataframe = character(0),
  columns = character(0),
  comment = character(0),
  stringsAsFactors = FALSE
)

add_comment <- function(df, comment) {
  df_name <- deparse(substitute(df))
  idx <- which(all_names$dataframe == df_name)
  if (length(idx) == 1) {
    if (all_names$comment[idx] == "") {
      all_names$comment[idx] <<- comment
    } else {
      all_names$comment[idx] <<- paste(all_names$comment[idx], comment, sep = "\n")
    }
  }
}


reset_fx <- function() {
  all_names <<- data.frame(dataframe = character(0), columns = character(0), comment = character(0), stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
}

df_comms <- function(df) {
  df_name <- deparse(substitute(df))
  idx <- which(all_names$dataframe == df_name)

  if (length(idx) == 0 || all_names$comment[idx] == "") {
    message("There are no comments currently for this dataframe.")
  } else {
    cat(all_names$comment[idx])
  }
}

curr_df <- function(env = .GlobalEnv) {
  objs <- ls(envir = env)
  df_objs <- Filter(function(x) {
    obj <- get(x, envir = env)
    inherits(obj, c("data.frame", "tbl_df", "data.table"))
  }, objs)

  if (length(df_objs) == 0) {
    message("No data-holding objects found.")
  } else {
    print(df_objs)
  }
}

misd_df <- function() {
  # Get current data-holding objects
  current_dfs <- curr_df()

  # If curr_df printed a message instead of returning a value
  if (is.null(current_dfs)) return(invisible(NULL))

  # Get already tracked dataframes
  tracked_dfs <- all_names$dataframe

  # Find untracked ones
  missing <- setdiff(current_dfs, tracked_dfs)

  if (length(missing) == 0) {
    message("All current dataframes are already tracked in all_names.")
  } else {
    cat("Untracked dataframes:\n")
    print(missing)
  }
}

  mutate(hrvSummary = map(hrvSummary, ~ {
    .x <- gsub("None", "null", .x)       # Replace Python None with JSON null
    .x <- gsub("'", "\"", .x)            # Replace single quotes with double quotes
    jsonlite::fromJSON(.x)                         # Parse JSON into named list
  })) %>%
  unnest_longer(hrvSummary, values_to = "value", indices_to = "key")

library(plotly)
library(dplyr)
library(lubridate)

# Prepare data
daty <- dg3_flat
fx(daty, "daty is identical to dg3_flat but used in a plot of HRV by time")
daty$readingTimeGMT <- as.POSIXct(daty$readingTimeGMT, format = "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%OS", tz = "UTC")
daty$readingTimeLocal <- with_tz(daty$readingTimeGMT, tzone = "America/Toronto")

# Define holidays
holidays <- as.Date(c("2025-01-01", "2025-07-01", "2025-12-25"))

# Create base scattergl plot
p <- plot_ly(
  data = daty,
  x = ~readingTimeLocal,
  y = ~hrvValue,
  type = "scattergl",
  mode = "markers",
  name = "HRV",
  marker = list(color = "#E74C3C", size = 3, opacity = 0.6)
)

holidays <- as.Date(c("2025-01-01", "2025-07-01", "2025-12-25"))
# Group by calendarDate to define vertical lines and labels
daily_lines <- daty %>%
  group_by(calendarDate) %>%
  summarise(
    x_start = min(readingTimeLocal),
    x_end = max(readingTimeLocal),
    .groups = "drop"
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    is_weekend = wday(calendarDate) %in% c(1, 7),
    is_holiday = calendarDate %in% holidays,
    label_color = ifelse(is_weekend | is_holiday, "#E74C3C", "#2980B9")
  )
fx(daily_lines, "daily_lines is a dataframe used in a plot of HRV to show weekends and holidays")
# Add vertical lines and date labels
max_y <- max(daty$hrvValue, na.rm = TRUE)

for (i in seq_len(nrow(daily_lines))) {
  p <- p %>%
    add_lines(
      x = c(daily_lines$x_start[i], daily_lines$x_start[i]),
      y = c(min(daty$hrvValue), max_y),
      line = list(color = "rgba(0,0,0,0.2)", dash = "dot"),
      showlegend = FALSE,
      inherit = FALSE
    ) %>%
    add_annotations(
      x = daily_lines$x_start[i],
      y = max_y + 5,
      text = as.character(daily_lines$calendarDate[i]),
      showarrow = FALSE,
      textangle = -90,
      font = list(size = 10, color = daily_lines$label_color[i])
    )
}

# Final layout
p <- p %>%
  layout(
    title = "Heart Rate Variability Over Time (HRV)",
    xaxis = list(title = "Time (EST)"),
    yaxis = list(title = "Heart Rate Variability (milliseconds)")
  )

p

calculate statistics Kruskall-Wallis ### Heart rate variability (HRV) was analyzed across 43 days, from August 20 to September 30. A Kruskal-Wallis test was used to compare HRV distributions between days, assessing whether any day showed a statistically different pattern. The test identified August 27 as significantly different from the others. In the box plot, August 27 is highlighted in red, while all other days are shown in blue, indicating a distinct physiological deviation on that date 
# Group by date and compute summary stats
stats <- daty %>%
  group_by(calendarDate) %>%
  summarise(
    Min = min(hrvValue, na.rm = TRUE),
    Q1 = quantile(hrvValue, 0.25, na.rm = TRUE),
    Median = median(hrvValue, na.rm = TRUE),
    Q3 = quantile(hrvValue, 0.75, na.rm = TRUE),
    Max = max(hrvValue, na.rm = TRUE),
    IQR = IQR(hrvValue, na.rm = TRUE),
    .groups = "drop"
  )
fx(stats, "stats created for Dunn's & Kruskall test and plots to compare HRV")
# Kruskal-Wallis test to detect significant difference
kruskal <- kruskal.test(hrvValue ~ calendarDate, data = daty)

# Identify the day with the highest median (as a proxy for anomaly)
anomaly_day <- stats %>%
  filter(Median == max(Median)) %>%
  pull(calendarDate)

# Add color flag
daty <- daty %>%
  mutate(colorFlag = ifelse(calendarDate == anomaly_day, "Anomaly", "Normal"))

# Plot using plotly
plot_ly(
  data = daty,
  x = ~calendarDate,
  y = ~hrvValue,
  type = "box",
  color = ~colorFlag,
  colors = c("Normal" = "#3498DB", "Anomaly" = "#E74C3C"),
  boxpoints = "all",
  jitter = 0.3,
  pointpos = 0
) %>%
  layout(
    title = "HRV Box Plot by Day with Anomaly Highlighted",
    xaxis = list(title = "Date"),
    yaxis = list(title = "HRV (ms)")
  )

statistics - Dunn's test The Dunn test is a post-hoc analysis used after a Kruskal-Wallis test to pinpoint which specific groups differ. In your case, it compares heart rate variability (HRV) across individual days. If all days are marked pink, it means the Dunn test found statistically significant differences in HRV between nearly every pair of days. This suggests that HRV fluctuated meaningfully from day to day, rather than following a stable or consistent pattern. 
#install.packages("FSA")
library(FSA)
results <- dunnTest(hrvValue ~ calendarDate, data = daty, method = "bh")

fx(dunn_results, "dunn_results is a table of stats results of comparing each HRV day against all others")
# Extract significantly different days
sig_days <- unique(c(dunn_results$Comparison[dunn_results$P.adj < 0.05]))
sig_days <- unique(unlist(strsplit(sig_days, " - ")))  # split pairs into individual days

# Assign color flags
daty <- daty %>%
  mutate(colorFlag = ifelse(calendarDate %in% sig_days, "Significant", "Normal"))

# Use ColorBrewer palette
colors <- c("Normal" = "#1f78b4", "Significant" = "#e31a1c")  # blue and red

plot_ly(
  data = daty,
  x = ~calendarDate,
  y = ~hrvValue,
  type = "box",
  color = ~colorFlag,
  colors = colors,
  boxpoints = "all",
  jitter = 0.25,
  pointpos = 0,
  marker = list(size = 2.5, opacity = 0.6)
) %>%
  layout(
    title = "HRV Box Plot by Day with Statistically Different Days Highlighted",
    xaxis = list(title = "Date"),
    yaxis = list(title = "HRV (ms)")
  )

# Make it a time-aware tibble
data_ts <- as_tbl_time(data, index = time)
fx(data_ts, "data_ts is a tibble version of data which contain bpm values; time ordered as an index")
# Apply anomaly detection
results <- data_ts %>%
  time_decompose(heartrate, method = "stl", frequency = "auto", trend = "auto") %>%
  anomalize(remainder, method = "iqr") %>%
  time_recompose()
fx(results, "results in a tbl_time that found anomalies in data_ts time series data")
str(results)

sho_tb <- function(df) {
  library(gt)

  df %>%
    head() %>%
    gt() %>%
    tab_header(
      title = "My GT Table",
      subtitle = "Styled with gt"
    )
}
sho_tb(tab)

########################### # this code shows the dates at the top and shows weekends in red for the bpm by date 
names(tab)

##  [1] "date"                             "observed"                        
##  [3] "season"                           "trend"                           
##  [5] "remainder"                        "remainder_l1"                    
##  [7] "remainder_l2"                     "anomaly"                         
##  [9] "recomposed_l1"                    "recomposed_l2"                   
## [11] "calendarDate"                     "maxHeartRate"                    
## [13] "minHeartRate"                     "restingHeartRate"                
## [15] "lastSevenDaysAvgRestingHeartRate"

# Convert anomaly column to logical (TRUE/FALSE)
tab$anomaly_flag <- tab$anomaly == "Yes"

# Define holidays (customize as needed)
holidays <- as.Date(c("2025-01-01", "2025-07-01", "2025-12-25"))  # Example: New Year, Canada Day, Christmas

# Create base scattergl plot
p <- plot_ly(
  data = tab,
  x = ~date,
  y = ~observed,
  type = "scattergl",
  mode = "markers",
  name = "Observed",
  marker = list(
    color = ifelse(tab$anomaly_flag, "#E74C3C", "#2C3E50"),
    size = 3,
    opacity = 0.6
  )
)

# Group by calendarDate
daily_lines <- tab %>%
  group_by(calendarDate) %>%
  summarise(
    x_start = min(date),
    x_end = max(date),
    maxHR = first(maxHeartRate),
    minHR = first(minHeartRate),
    restHR = first(restingHeartRate)
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    is_weekend = wday(calendarDate) %in% c(1, 7),  # Sunday = 1, Saturday = 7
    is_holiday = calendarDate %in% holidays,
    label_color = ifelse(is_weekend | is_holiday, "#E74C3C", "#2980B9")
  )

# Add horizontal lines
p <- p %>%
  add_segments(
    data = daily_lines,
    x = ~x_start, xend = ~x_end,
    y = ~maxHR, yend = ~maxHR,
    line = list(color = "#E67E22", width = 2),
    name = "Max HR", inherit = FALSE
  ) %>%
  add_segments(
    data = daily_lines,
    x = ~x_start, xend = ~x_end,
    y = ~minHR, yend = ~minHR,
    line = list(color = "#3498DB", width = 2),
    name = "Min HR", inherit = FALSE
  ) %>%
  add_segments(
    data = daily_lines,
    x = ~x_start, xend = ~x_end,
    y = ~restHR, yend = ~restHR,
    line = list(color = "#2ECC71", width = 2),
    name = "Resting HR", inherit = FALSE
  )

# Add vertical lines and colored date labels
max_y <- max(tab$observed, na.rm = TRUE)

for (i in seq_len(nrow(daily_lines))) {
  p <- p %>%
    add_lines(
      x = c(daily_lines$x_start[i], daily_lines$x_start[i]),
      y = c(min(tab$observed), max_y),
      line = list(color = "rgba(0,0,0,0.2)", dash = "dot"),
      showlegend = FALSE,
      inherit = FALSE
    ) %>%
    add_annotations(
      x = daily_lines$x_start[i],
      y = max_y + 5,
      text = as.character(daily_lines$calendarDate[i]),
      showarrow = FALSE,
      textangle = -90,
      font = list(size = 10, color = daily_lines$label_color[i])
    )
}

# Final layout
p <- p %>%
  layout(
    title = "Heart Rate Anomaly Detection with Daily Metrics",
    xaxis = list(title = "Time (UTC)"),
    yaxis = list(title = "Heart Rate (bpm)")
  )

p

kruskall for heart rate (bpm) Heart rate (bpm) was analyzed across 43 days, from August 20 to September 30. A Kruskal-Wallis test was used to compare heart distributions between days, assessing whether any day showed a statistically different pattern. The test identified Sept 24 as significantly different from the others. In the box plot, Sept 24 is highlighted in red, while all other days are shown in blue, indicating a distinct physiological deviation on that date. Also, incidentally the resting heart rate was higher than that of all other days.

# Kruskal-Wallis test across calendarDate
kruskal <- kruskal.test(observed ~ calendarDate, data = tab)

# Identify anomalous day with highest median
anomaly_day <- stats %>%
  filter(Median == max(Median)) %>%
  pull(calendarDate)

# Add color flag to tab
tab <- tab %>%
  mutate(colorFlag = ifelse(calendarDate == anomaly_day, "Anomaly", "Normal"))


# Box plot with anomaly highlight
plot_ly(
  data = tab,
  x = ~calendarDate,
  y = ~observed,
  type = "box",
  color = ~colorFlag,
  colors = c("Normal" = "#3498DB", "Anomaly" = "#E74C3C"),
  boxpoints = "all",
  jitter = 0.3,
  pointpos = 0
) %>%
  layout(
    title = "Heart Rate Box Plot by Day with Anomaly Highlighted (Kruskal-Wallis)",
    xaxis = list(title = "Date"),
    yaxis = list(title = "Heart Rate (bpm)")
  )
